The class, found by execution on the text upstream landed from the fork's offer 1847: a read the hook makes of the pushed objects fails, or returns less than the objects hold, and the hook reads the result as an empty one, so a part-scanned push passes as a clean one. The fork's fix routes every such read through one helper (unscanned: one stderr line naming the ref or the commit and the read, failed_scan=1, the push refused on that flag alone; failed_creds gates the gitleaks advice) or lets the read's own status end the hook; an ABSENT value (an empty target, a tag with no tagger or message, a lightweight tag, a blob or tree tag, a gitlink, a file binary by its bytes, a commit whose diff or message is empty) still reads as empty and passes, and every hit path prints the same text and line numbers as before (the round f4 and f5 contract runs, re-run in r1 and again after the round's audit: byte-identical stderr on the r1 text and its predecessor over 30 shapes, the one difference bash's own null-byte warning naming a moved line). Nine roads are closed in the fold, found one at a time and then by derivation: round f3 the tag fields; round f4 the tip's content grep, its symlink listing, each link's target and the tag TYPE read; round f5 the per-commit added lines, addresses and message; review round r1 the credential scan's read INSIDE gitleaks (the eighth road, below), beside it two holes of the identifier scan's own that the f5 derivation had called closed or not seen (the denylist reader dropped the file's last entry when the file had no trailing newline, every read succeeding and the loop discarding data, the shape that widened the class; and the parent count's failure was reported as the diff-tree's), and, found OPEN by the round's derivation on the r1 fixer's text and closed by the round's audit, the colour road (the ninth, below): a `git log -p` stream the clone's own colour configuration had emptied for the scanner and for the hook's count alike. Review round r2 then found that the colour road was one instance of a CLASS, transforms of a stream both readers shared (six found in two rounds, below), and closed the class structurally rather than transform by transform: the hook's count is derived from the OBJECTS (rev-list, diff-tree --raw, cat-file), which no diff attribute, driver, colour, pretty or date key touches, so a diff-level transform can no longer silence the scan and its check alike; and the premise both scans rest on, that what they read is what the push transfers, is stated at the hook and enforced (replacement off for every git the hook runs, and a refusal under any replace ref, the one shape found where the two diverge). Round r2c then narrowed that refusal to where a scan would run (two arming predicates, identifier_scan_armed and credential_scan_armed, which the scans' own early returns read as well, so the gate and the scans agree by construction: the refusal prevents a false clean report, and where no scan runs there is no report to falsify, so a clone that asked for neither scan stays unaffected, as CLAUDE.md promises) and closed the identifier scan's own instance of the class, a TEXT blob whose path's diff attribute makes git's grep and diff call it binary and skip it, by REFUSAL rather than by scanning (refuse_hidden_paths: the diff attribute of every blob the scan would read, in one git check-attr per ref, and a hidden blob whose bytes are text named with the tip or the commit, the path and the attribute; a blob binary by its bytes stays the documented pass); reading such a blob from the objects is the better long fix, named by the ruling and not taken in this fold. Rounds r2e and r2h (one commit) then took the r2c flags as ruled (the attribute check's scratch directory removed by its exact minted path with the residual stated at the header, the advice naming the attribute as the cause with the explicit-diff remedy and the rename clause) and corrected a premise: log.showRoot set to false, named at r2 and r2c as the one transform no option reaches and so a fail-closed over-refusal, IS reached by an option (`--root`, found by execution in r2e), and since r2h that option in gitleaks_args neutralises the key, so a root commit's diff is scanned and a clean root push passes; the refusal arm stays for a scanner whose git does not honour the option, naming the key, the option and the remedy (the class paragraph below; since r3 the arm states the two facts it observed and names the key as a CANDIDATE cause, since a log shortened for any other reason meets the same two facts). Review round r3 (2026-09-21) then found the identifier half's r2c closure FALSE by execution: it was keyed on the diff attribute's VALUE, and two shapes outside that enumeration published through real pushes with the denylist armed and nothing printed, a text blob over core.bigFileThreshold in a middle commit (git prints no hunk for it and check-attr answers unspecified) and a text blob under a driver named one of check-attr's reserved words, `set` or `unspecified`, with diff.<driver>.binary true (the value arm read the word as no attribute). The round replaced the enumeration with a DERIVED PROPERTY, git's own verdict on the read each half makes (refuse_hidden_paths since r3: the tip's grep asked what it read, the per-commit diff's numstat over the pairs it reads, a blob so named judged by its bytes), and rounds r3c to r3f (2026-09-22) made the derivation's scope equal the property's on every shape the auditors' real pushes tried (symlink post-images and type changes, r3c; a merge judged by its own combined patch and the tip owning its blobs, r3d; a header-only section a candidate and the binary-to-text cause line, r3e; a merge's third-path rename, r3f), each round's witness named in the identifier-half paragraph below. Round r3g (2026-09-22) then closed the one instance of the class the r3b derivation had found in the hook's own scratch-file tools and recorded as a residual: as_lines' rewrites and newline tests each read their own status, so a tr or wc that fails on one listing and runs on the next refuses the push as unscanned at each caller, naming the tip or the commit and the tool, where the r3f text handed the join an empty rewrite with no status to read (the tip half judging nothing over a hidden text file the remote already held; a commit with a banned string under -diff passing unjudged; a merge's short read claimed for the wrong cause; four bats cases, each red at the r3f text by execution). Review round r5 (2026-09-22, romp-manager's round 4 rulings) then found, on the r3g text, that the fail-closed guarantee covered the STATUS-KEYED class alone (a read whose failure returns a non-zero status) and that a second class stood open, the EXIT-0 EMPTY-OR-SHORT-ANSWER class: a tool exiting 0 with nothing, or with less than the objects hold, left paths unread and the push passing (the per-commit changed-path listing answering nothing, the tip's -z listing answering nothing or dropping a record, a rewrite's tr writing nothing, a cat-file printing nothing for a blob with bytes: four shapes, each publishing a hidden text file through a REAL push at the r3g text), where the hook had gated it read by read for the numstats and check-attr alone since round 3; the round gated the CLASS, every such read checked against a second read already in hand and a disagreement refusing the push naming the read and both answers (refuse_hidden_paths since r5: the listing against the paths its verdicts name, the tip's listing against the symlink pass's count and the grep's read list, each rewrite's byte count against its input's with every count validated as digits, the byte judge's zero against the blob's size), the header stating the guarantee per class and the bound of the second (the content reads' own empty answers have no sibling read and stay as absent); found the three arms of rounds r3 to r3f without an executed pin, every case keeping the blob at the pushed tip where the tip-owns rule set it aside before the arm was reached, and gave each a case with the blob ABSENT from the tip, red with the arm disabled; and closed the merge twin and the rename twin of the disclosed binary-to-text shape, the previous version read in each parent of a merge and at a rename's old path so every refusal of that class names its cause and the fetch remedy. Round r5b (2026-09-22, the round 4 lows) then read the added lines through a scratch file rather than a substitution (bash's null-byte warning gone from a real push of this repository's history), printed each advice paragraph only where a refused line named its cause, keyed the two-fact line's tail on the attribute read's outcome, named the path in the three short-read lines, and gave the header's unquoting and a three-parent merge their cases. Review round r6 (2026-09-22, romp-manager's round 5 rulings, group A) then found six more reads of the second class on the r5b text, each shown by a real push with a shim publishing a banned line with nothing printed (a grep exiting 2 over the added lines, a symlink target, an address domain and a message, read as no match; the commit listing answering nothing on a new branch and on a ref update, the per-commit loop running zero times; a parent count of nothing beside an empty combined listing, the merge read as a one-parent commit; a type of nothing over a banned tagger, read as not a tag; an empty symlink target over a blob with bytes, read as an empty target), where round 5 had gated the class read by read and the header had listed the gated reads by hand, and closed BOTH classes BY CONSTRUCTION rather than with six more patches: every read the hook judges goes through one helper, judged_read, which captures the status and the output together and refuses any status outside the read's expected set as unscanned naming the read (the status-keyed class), and every call carries a tag saying how an exit-0 empty answer is judged, gate= where a sibling fact in hand must agree with it or own= where the empty answer is the object's own (the second class); the header's two lists are DERIVED from those tags and pinned by a bats case that reads the tags out of the hook (a name added, dropped or edited by hand, or a call without a tag, is red there), and a read left outside the helper is declared at its site with the reason. Round r6b (2026-09-22, groups B to E) then marked +typed only where the addition verdict decided, so a merge's rename candidate whose combined section printed Binary takes the parent loop and names the parent's version with the fetch remedy and no key advice; printed both remedies on a previous-version line, the fetch and an explicit diff attribute on the PREVIOUS version's path (the old path for a rename), which lifts the pair to text by execution; gave the join a fourth arm for its own tools' failure, the helper's clause naming the pipeline's status, the tool's error line above and whether the join had recorded a path (a rider composed after the read through the helper's -d option), the three short-read lines keyed on 3 and 4; passed the short file's path to awk through the environment, never -v; and disclosed the newline widening (a commit that only deletes a newline-named path the remote holds is refused too). Review round r7 (2026-09-23, romp-manager's round 6 rulings, group A) then found, on the r6b text and by real pushes with a tool exiting 0 and printing nothing, that round 5's ruling that the exit-0 class was closed by construction held for the gate= reads alone: five own= sites and the ref list itself still published (the MESSAGE grep over a commit's subject and a tag's message, under a silent grep and under the REAL grep over a byte that is no UTF-8 in a UTF-8 locale; the TIP CANDIDATES join under a silent awk and a silent tr; the JOIN of a commit's verdicts under either; the ADDED LINES diff under a silent awk; a tag's TAGGER field and MESSAGE under an awk silent for their programs, and a commit's MESSAGE under a git silent on its format; every ref of a push under a silent cat), a capture file the helper could not open published with bash's own line the only sign, and the ADDED LINES grep's exit-0 silence was refused with the sort named as the cause: thirteen publications in one round, none printing a hook line. Round r7 closed them (the class paragraphs and the rows below): the MESSAGE grep refuses a match that printed no hit line, reads with -a and is gate=; tip_candidates is gated on a count in hand, the -z listing's regular files with bytes less the grep's read list, against the rows it appended, counted by their NUL terminators in the shell (nul_records); join_candidates and added_lines each record the count of rows they printed in a count file the caller compares with the rows appended or the capture's line count; the tag object is read ONCE through judged_read -o, judged against its size when empty, and its tagger, message and object line are parsed by the shell (tag_field, tag_message and tag_unread retired); a commit's MESSAGE log asks for a marker line ahead of the message and is judged against it; the ref list is read by the shell's own builtin with no tool between git and the loops; judged_read opens its -o or -a file once to a descriptor before the command and refuses a failed open whatever the expected set; the ADDED LINES grep's empty hits under exit 0 are refused before the sort runs; and the header's own= paragraph became the DERIVED BOUND, naming what stays own= and why a tool silent there publishes nothing by itself (no own= read is a read of an object's content since). Round r7b (2026-09-23, groups B to D) then pinned the POPULATION half of the guarantee (a census case over the hook's text: every line running a reading tool is a judged_read call, a line inside a function the helper is handed whole, an array-literal assignment or a line under an `outside judged_read:` marker; red on a planted read, green at the head), routed the chosen-addresses read through the helper (own=, called in the shell, the address lines leading with the failed read where it failed, where the swallowed failure had printed a false cause), quoted the -d rider's splice and substituted {rc} first, and gave the odd tag object and the ENTRY COUNT grep's truly silent shape their cases. One offer for the whole class with the harness; the offer is authorised and sequenced after the fork landing (the closing paragraph).

The eighth road, stated as executed (the round f5 entry stated it too narrowly, as gitleaks reading its git log FAILURE as an empty scan, and a fix built to that spec would still have failed open): gitleaks 8.30.1 (and 8.28.0, the version CI pins) runs `git -C <root> log -p -U0 <range> --diff-merges=first-parent` itself, and aborts its scan on the FIRST stderr line from that git that is not one it allowlists as a rename-detection warning (by execution: the two rename-detection warnings and the `diff.renameLimit` hint pass; the source names the auto-gc notice too), WHATEVER git's exit status: it logs `ERR [git] <line>` and `ERR error="stderr is not empty"`, drops the rest of the scan, reports the commits it did read as `N commits scanned`, says `no leaks found` for every finding not already emitted, and exits 0. The commit count and byte count it prints may still be the full expected ones (three commits parsed, the noise on the oldest, `3 commits scanned` equal to `git rev-list --count`, a live token in the range, `no leaks found`), so neither git's exit status nor an agreeing commit count distinguishes an aborted scan from a clean one. The hook at f5 classified on the exit status alone (`case $rc in 0) ;;`), so exit 0 read as CLEAN and the credential published, with nothing printed but gitleaks' own lines. The road is open on every clone, the maintainer's included: with the denylist armed, no read of the identifier arm fails in these modes (`git diff-tree` is plumbing and applies no textconv; `git grep` reads the raw blobs), so the f5 entry's sentence that the denylist masked the road was false. The executed fault modes, each a REAL push through the installed hook with the denylist armed and a credential assembled at run time, each publishing at the f5 text with zero hook lines:

- a git that writes ONE benign line to stderr and otherwise runs unchanged (a shim on gitleaks' invocation shape; romp-manager's own reproduction): `ERR [git] ...`, `1 commits scanned` of two, `no leaks found`, exit 0; two live credentials published.
- a content-preserving textconv that exits 0 and writes one stderr line (`.gitattributes` `diff=<name>` plus `diff.<name>.textconv`; a personal `core.attributesFile` reaches it too): git log -p exits 0; gitleaks reports the FULL count (1 of 1, 2 of 2, 3 of 3, agreeing with `git rev-list --count` every time), `no leaks found`, exit 0; the token on the remote. This is the mode that defeats a readability probe (`git log -p ... >/dev/null || unscanned` passes: git exited 0) and a count comparison alone.
- a textconv whose converter is missing: git log -p exits 128 (`fatal: cannot exec`), `git diff-tree` exits 0 and `git grep` 1 for the identifier arm; gitleaks `0 commits scanned`, exit 0; published.
- a git that rejects `--diff-merges=first-parent` (any git below 2.31): `ERR [git] error: unknown option`, `0 commits scanned`, exit 0; published, every identifier-arm read succeeding.
- a merge whose side branch the remote already holds, the credential in the plain commit before the merge, the side branch's dropped file's blob unlinked from the store: `git grep` 1, `git ls-tree` 0, `git diff-tree` 0 on both new commits (the merge's combined diff is empty), gitleaks' git log -p 128 (`unable to read`), `1 commits scanned`, exit 0; published. (The f5 entry's shape, the added file's blob gone from the tip's own commit, makes diff-tree exit 128 and the f5 ADDED LINES read refuses first; the shape matters, and this one is the reachable one.)
- a log that comes up short with no stderr line at all (gitleaks' git given `--max-count=1`, the secret in the older of two commits): `1 commits scanned`, `no leaks found`, exit 0; published. No ERR line is written in this mode; only the count condition sees it.
- a git that exits 128 inside gitleaks (`fatal: bad object`), and the f5 entry's fallback shape (a range this clone resolves whose walk then fails): `ERR [git] fatal: ...`, `0 commits scanned`, exit 0; published.
- the controls: two live secrets with a healthy git are refused as a finding (exit 2, both rules named, redacted); git's rename-limit warnings (`diff.renameLimit=1` over twelve renames) are logged at WRN and the finding is still reported, exit 2.
- not executed, by assessment: a transient fault inside gitleaks' own process (a signal, EMFILE) is not reproducible by a separate probe of git and is visible only in gitleaks' own log, which is why the remedy reads that log rather than probing git again.

The fix, in the fold (review round r1; the rule: stop inferring "clean" from exit 0, which says gitleaks reported no finding, not that it scanned; require positive evidence of coverage and refuse without it). The hook captures gitleaks' stderr, its log, apart from its stdout (the findings still stream live to the hook's stderr through a third descriptor: `log=$( { "$gl" git ... 2>&1 1>&3; } 3>&2 ) || rc=$?`), re-emits the log, keeps the exit-status arms (1 or above 2: failed_scan and failed_creds as before; 2: a finding), and when gitleaks CLAIMS a scan (exit 0 or 2) applies two independent conditions to an SGR-stripped copy of the log: (1) any line at its ERR level refuses, `the CREDENTIAL scan of <ref> (<sha10>) did not complete: gitleaks logged an error (<the first ERR line>)`, failed_creds=1; (2) the reported `<n> commits scanned.` must equal the hook's own count of the commits with content to scan, `covered <reported> of the <expected> commits with content to scan` on a mismatch, `reported no commit count, so what it read is unknown` when the line is absent, failed_creds=1 on each. Two conditions because either alone has a hole: the short mode writes no ERR line, and an aborted scan can report the whole count. At the r1 text the expected count was derived by a new helper, scannable_commits, over the SAME `git log -p -U0 --format='commit %H' --diff-merges=first-parent` stream by gitleaks' own rule (a commit counts once a file that is neither deleted nor binary has a hunk; gitleaks' sources for v8.30.1 and v8.28.0 read: one `addCommit` per fragment, a fragment one hunk of a non-deleted non-binary file, the count the distinct shas), because `git rev-list --count` over-counts every empty, delete-only, binary-only, mode-only or rename-only commit and every merge with an empty first-parent diff (nine of 22 shapes disagree with gitleaks; that derivation agreed with both gitleaks versions on all 22 and on a 4556-commit range of the fork's own history, 4544 both). The same stream, but not the same bytes: the r1 audit had both reads ask git for a plain stream, `git log --no-color` in scannable_commits and `--no-color` appended to gitleaks' `--log-opts`, because a transform the clone's configuration applies to the stream reaches both readers alike and a count that agrees with an emptied scan is no evidence (the colour road, below). Review round r2 found five more transforms of that stream and replaced the derivation: scannable_commits now reads the OBJECTS and consults no stream (the class paragraph below), and gitleaks' options live in one place, gitleaks_args. The derivation's own failure is its own road, read before the scan: `expected=$(scannable_commits $range) || { rc=$?; expected=""; }` with the clause `the COMMITS of <ref> (<sha10>) could not be counted for the credential scan (a git read exited N)` (`git log exited N` at r1), failed_creds=1; since r6 the read goes through judged_read (gate: the COUNT of commits with content to scan, the same clause with {rc} the status) and its answer is judged as a run of digits, an exit-0 answer of any other shape refused as not a count (the count's awk answering nothing beside a scanner handed a shorter log PASSED at the r5b text, the comparison skipped on the empty count: bats case 185 with the real scanner, red there), as are the repository root (2405), the range probe (2433) and the three reads of the scanner's log (2486, 2491, 2500), each with the scanner's advice on a refusal where the r5b text ended the hook by errexit with no line. The rev-list probe's comment now says what it covers (an UNRESOLVABLE range only; what gitleaks did with the range is read from its log, not its exit status), and the hook's header paragraph states the road, the two-condition remedy and the colour road. The invocation gained that one argument at r1, `--no-color` after `--diff-merges=first-parent` inside `--log-opts` (nine plain-stream options since r2 and ten since r2h, `--root` the tenth, built by gitleaks_args); tests/install-sh.bats's pins on the invocation are substring globs and still match (its stub strips the `--log-opts` value after the range and `--diff-merges=first-parent`), and its stub scanner now reports the range's commit count in gitleaks' own line shape, since the hook requires the count as positive evidence (the harness paragraph). Verified by execution with gitleaks 8.30.1 and 8.28.0, the hook fed its ref line directly and through REAL pushes: the noise mode is refused on both conditions (the ERR line, and `covered 0 of the 2` or `1 of the 2`: the abort's count varies with the race between gitleaks' two readers, and both refuse), the short mode by the count alone (`covered 1 of the 2`), the bad-object and fallback modes by both, the rename warning and the two-secret control still as a finding, the remote unchanged in every refusal; a clean push prints byte-identical output to the f5 text's; on a finding one INF line moves (the log is shown after the live findings). In tests/pre-push-hook.bats, the section "the credential scan's own read: gitleaks' log" (the real scanner, skipped where gitleaks is absent as tests/gitleaks-config.bats skips; the probe tokens assembled at run time; a second generic shim, `git_passing_through <bash test over "$@"> <stderr line or empty> [<appended argument>]`, keyed on gitleaks' invocation shape and no read of the hook's own, with the wrappers gitleaks_git_noisy, gitleaks_git_short and gitleaks_git_warning): the two-secret control, a clean pass with the count shown as before, the no-content shapes (at r1 an empty commit, a deletion, a binary add: four commits by rev-list, `1 commits scanned`, no hook line: a pin on the derivation rule, red on a hook that counted with rev-list; re-cut at r2 for the resolved invocation, under which a binary add and a pure rename count as their bytes say: seven commits, three counted), the ERR mode and the short mode (each red at the f5 text at `[ "$status" -eq 1 ]`: the fail-open), the allowlisted rename warning as a WRN pass control, and the two colour cases (below). Round r3 added, in the same section, the count's two remaining arms with the REAL scanner (a blob the size read cannot find fails the count with the exact clause `a git read exited 3`, where under ROMP_NO_GITLEAKS the count never runs and with the scanner a looser assertion stays green through gitleaks' own ERR arm; an ADDED gitlink whose commit this store lacks passes, counted by both, pinning the `$2 != "160000"` filter of the sizes read, without which the gitlink's absent commit reads `missing` and a clean submodule push is refused; bats cases 46 and 47, red under mutants m10 and m11), and in the showRoot section the one case that holds WHERE the count comes from (round r3's ruling C): a git shim stripping `--root` from every invocation carrying the word `log` and from no diff-tree (git_stripping_root_from_log), log.showRoot false, a credential in the root commit, asserting status 1, `covered 1 of the 2 commits with content to scan` and the key line (case 74); a count derived from an equally hardened `git log -p` stream, the shape round r2's ruling excluded, agrees with the shortened scan and passes it (exit 0, the credential published: mutant m12, red on case 74 alone, where 87 of the 88 cases at 89589e7d3 stayed green on that mutant), since every plain-stream option that neutralises a key for the scanner's git neutralises it for such a count alike, so the thirteen transform cases hold that each named transform is closed and not the derivation; the file's header, the transform section's comment and the count-read case's comment (case 54 since r3, r1's 52: its shim keys on the count read's own shape, `diff-tree --stdin`, not on the derivation) say so since r3.

The property in one sentence: no read the hook makes of the pushed objects, its own or the one inside gitleaks, can fail or come up short in a way the hook cannot tell from an empty result, because each read's status is captured before its output is judged, a non-zero status refuses the push as unscanned, and the one read whose status carries nothing is judged from the scanner's own log against a count the hook derives itself from the objects, which no transform of the patch stream reaches; and the objects both scans read are the ones the push transfers, replacement off, or the push is refused before either scan (where a scan runs at all: a clone that asked for neither makes no report a replace ref could falsify); and a blob the identifier scan's two content reads SKIPPED as binary, by git's own verdict on each read (the tip's grep asked what it read; the per-commit diff's numstat over the pair it reads, or for a merge its combined patch itself), whose bytes are text, is refused rather than read as empty, whatever rule made git call it binary (its path's diff attribute, core.bigFileThreshold, a driver under any name, a rule a later git adds), less a blob the pushed tip's tree holds, which the tip half judges. The guarantee is of two KINDS, stated per class at the hook's header since r5 and closed BY CONSTRUCTION since r6: every read the hook judges (every git whose output a check reads, every grep over captured content, every tool a join runs: 56 call lines carrying 52 distinct tags since r7b; 53 and 49 at r6b) goes through one helper, judged_read, which captures the status and the output together (read_rc and read_out, or a file for a NUL-delimited listing and for the added lines, which a substitution would mangle) and refuses any status outside the read's EXPECTED set as unscanned naming the read, {rc} in the caller's clause replaced by the status (a grep: 0 and 1; a git: 0; the set stated at each call, with the reason where it is wider: 0 and 128 for the symlink listing, whose 128 is a pushed object that peels to a blob, and for the commit listing and the range probe, whose 128 is a range this clone cannot resolve; 0, 3 and 4 for the join, 3 and 4 its short-read codes), the STATUS-KEYED class; and the EXIT-0 EMPTY-OR-SHORT-ANSWER class, a read that exits 0 with an empty or a short answer the check would read as nothing to judge, which has no status to read and is answered at each call site by the TAG the helper is given: gate= where a second read or fact already in hand must agree with the answer, named at the call site, a disagreement refusing the push as unscanned naming the read and both answers (42 names since r7b, 32 at r6b: the content grep of the tip against its own status, a match that printed no hit line being short; the symlink listing against the peeled type; a symlink target read as empty against the blob's size; the commit listing answering nothing against the peeled type, else against the remote-tracking refs containing the pushed commit OR the pushed commit's ancestry over the remote's; the parent count against the commit named first; the addresses log against its two role lines; a type against the object types; a tag's object field against its type; the -z listing against the symlink pass's entry count and the grep's read list; each rewrite's byte count against its input's, every count a run of digits; the changed-path listing against the paths its verdicts name; the combined patch, its section rows, the per-parent listing, the rename candidates, the numstats and the empty tree's name against the join, where a post-image no verdict answers for is a short read; the byte judge's count of zero against the blob's size; the sort's answer against the lines it was given; the repository root and the count of commits with content against their shape; the scanner log's colour strip and count line against the count arm; since r7 the message grep and the added-lines grep against their own status, a match that printed no hit line being short; the tip candidates join against the -z listing's regular files with bytes less the grep's read list, the file count and the line count read apart and each a run of digits; the join of a commit's verdicts against the count its awk recorded of the rows it printed; the added-lines diff against its awk's count and the capture's line count; the message log against the marker line its format asks for ahead of the message; the tag object, read once, against its size when the capture is empty, and its object line's presence against the type read), or own= where the empty answer is the object's own and no sibling fact exists, so it passes as absent (10 names since r7b, 17 at r6b: the address-domain grep, the chosen-address match and, since r7b, the chosen-addresses read, the symlink-target grep, the replace-refs listing, the tip listing join against the read list, the tip blobs and the listing join of a commit against its verdicts, the scanner log's ERR line, the range probe; the seven that left the list in r7 are gate= or retired: the added-lines diff and its grep, the message log and its grep, the tip candidates join, the join of a commit's verdicts, a tag's tagger field and message). The header's two lists are DERIVED from those tags, not written by hand: tests/pre-push-hook.bats case 174 reads every tag out of the hook and fails when either list names anything else, misses a tag, or a call carries none (red under four header mutants: a name dropped from the own list, a name nobody tags added, a gate name edited by hand, a call site stripped of its tag; since r7b red too with the chosen-addresses tag removed from the own= list), and since r7b case 203 pins the POPULATION half: a census over the hook's text finds no command line running a reading tool (the vocabulary stated in the case as its bound) that is not a judged_read call, inside a function the helper is handed whole, an array-literal assignment or under an `outside judged_read:` marker, quoted text masked quote-aware so code inside a command substitution stays visible, `|| true` and `|| :` inside a passed-whole body listed as a second bound (red on a planted `tipname=$(git rev-parse ...)` in the ref loop, the round 6 refuters' plant, naming the planted line, and on its double-quoted spelling, the second refuter's bypass; green at 47b595b26 and at the head, 28 command lines read, 17 in passed bodies, 10 under markers, 1 array literal, 0 undeclared). The second class's BOUND is the own= list: a tool that exits 0 while printing nothing at one of those own= reads (seventeen at r6b, ten since r7b) is outside the guarantee by design, since no sibling fact exists to check it against (round 5 had listed the gated reads by hand and named four content reads as the bound, the r5 hook fixer's FLAG 2 and the r5 audit's finding 3; round 6's refuters found six reads of the class outside that list, each publishing through a real push, which is why the list is derived now; the residual paragraph names the five joins among the seventeen at r6b and the three among the ten since r7b). Since r7 the own= list is the DERIVED BOUND stated at the header: no own= read is a read of an object's CONTENT (round 6's refuters found five own= reads and the ref list publishing under a tool exiting 0 printing nothing, each by a real push, thirteen publications, and round 7 made each of those reads gate= or read it by the shell), and what stays own= is named there with why a tool silent at it publishes nothing by itself: three -q greps whose answer is their status alone; the replace-ref listing and the chosen-addresses read, git reads alone whose empty answer is a clone with no replace ref or no user.email, the strict side; three joins over the scratch listings whose empty answer is the clean verdict of a gate on ANOTHER read or the safe side, so a tool silent there disarms that one gate while the read it gates stands under its status and its other gates; the scanner log's error line beside the gated count line; and the range probe, whose status alone is read; the residual the bound leaves outside the guarantee is two tools silent at once, a listing and its gate (the residual paragraph, with the r7 hook fixer's FLAG 2 on the ruling's closing sentence, that four own= reads still run an awk). A read left outside the helper is declared at its site with the reason (`outside judged_read:`, TWELVE declared reads at the r7b text, counted from the markers: the eight reads made for the report alone, whose failure leaves a refused line with fewer facts and decides nothing (the label's check-attr and the driver key, the blob's size and the threshold key, the parent count's awk over the hook's own pcount file, a read of no object, the rename listing and the previous version's bytes in a parent and in each parent of a merge); one configuration read, the clone's log.showRoot, whose swallowed failure costs an advice line alone and is pinned as such; the ref list on stdin, read by the shell's own builtin since r7; the scanner's own exit, judged from its log; the root-commit probe of the advice arm. Thirteen at the r6b text, where the chosen-addresses read was declared too, through the helper since r7b; this entry had counted twelve there with seven report reads, an undercount of the report's reads by one, since the awk over the hook's own parent-count scratch file is a read command 1 lists no row for, extra9-4: the population below counts it and names it, and its partition sums with twelve at the r7b text, ten of them command 1's rows and two, the ref list's shell read and that awk, command 2's alone).

The derivation, re-run in round r1 over the fixed text, again over that round's committed text after its audit's repair (the colour road, below), again in review round r2 over the committed text with the object derivation, again in round r2c over the committed text with the narrowed gate and the attribute refusal, again in round r2f over the text rounds r2e and r2h committed (the `--root` option, the showroot_configured read, the scratch removal's separator, the two advice lines), and again in round r3b over the text rounds r3 to r3f committed (the binary-verdict check as it stands, e94e92ffb), and again in round r3g over the hook as staged in that round (as_lines' status arms and its callers' branches), and again in round r5c over the text rounds r5 and r5b committed (381754fe4, the hook at the upkeep merge 8df5520aa: the second class's gates, the arms' pins off the tip, the previous version in each parent and at a rename's old path, the added lines through a file, the advice and the short-read wording), and again in round r6c over the text rounds r6 and r6b committed (0fbfcd0d7, the hook at the upkeep merge ac65b0b55: every read through judged_read, the six reads of group A gated, the joins as named functions, the r6b changes), and again in round r7c over the text rounds r7 and r7b committed (753edb3ad, the hook at HEAD: the five round 7 gates and the shell reads of the ref list and the tag object, the capture opened once, the chosen-addresses read through the helper, the quoted -d splice), the commands unchanged (the population below is the r7b text's, the r6b number beside each row, with the r1, r2, r2c, r2e, r3f, r3g, r5b and r6b figures kept in the two command paragraphs), with two commands. Command 1 is the round f5 command, run from the repo root; it lists every statement of the hook that names an external read (git, cat, command -v, the gitleaks binary, a helper wrapping one), a judge grep, a file test or the denylist redirect, joined across backslash continuations with comments stripped, and tags each with its syntactic shape (a suppressed, b a substitution with no arm, c the left side of a pipe, d inside a test, s a status read or a refusing arm, R a read, j a judge grep, f a file test, i the input redirect, p report text). Two changes for the r1 text: the two r1 helpers, parent_count and scannable_commits, join the read and pipe-left alternations so their call sites are listed (their inner git reads were caught already), and the arm shape `|| { rc=$?; ... }` joins the status regex. No change for the r2 text: the two new helpers' inner reads are listed on their own statements (refuse_replace_refs' for-each-ref; scannable_commits' two pipelines), and gitleaks_args' argument list is listed once as a false positive (it names git without running it). No change for the r2c text either: refuse_hidden_paths' five reads are plain git statements (ls-tree, diff-tree, check-attr, config, cat-file), each listed on its own line with its status arm, and its loop closers are input redirects, which the command lists; the two predicates' bodies are listed through the file test and the command -v that moved into them; the predicates' call sites and refuse_hidden_paths' own call are not listed, which is right, since none is a read (the helper returns 0 and every read inside it carries its own arm, so its closure is at the helper, not at a caller); mktemp, rm, head and od join the vocabulary cross-check (below). No change for the r2e text either: the showroot_configured read is a plain git statement at the top level, listed as a suppressed substitution with no arm (the one new row); the arm's test reads that variable in place of the config substitution it carried at r2c and is still listed through its rev-list substitution; gitleaks_args' list is listed once as before, `--root` inside its string; the two advice lines, like the r2c advice line, name no read and are not listed. No change for the r3f text either: the check's new git reads are plain git statements, each listed on its own line with its status arm (the tip's `ls-tree -r -z -l` and its read-list grep; a merge's combined patch and its per-parent listing; the empty tree's name; numstat_rows' one diff-tree, listed once at the helper for its three call sites, which read its status like scannable_commits' callers and are not listed themselves, the helper's name being in neither alternation); the label's reads (check-attr per refused path, the driver key, cat-file -s, the config key, the previous version's parent count and bytes) are listed as suppressed reads with no arm and judged safe in the population below; the seven refusing arms of the shape `[ "$rc" -eq 0 ] || { numstat_ok=0; unscanned ... }` and the three `says=` label clauses name git in their text and are listed as R rows the p pattern does not match, set aside as report text below; as_lines' tr and wc and combined_rows' awk run over the hook's own scratch files and are reads of no object (command 2 lists as_lines' statements as truncating filters and substitutions, two at the r3f text, four since r3g and twelve since r5, each with its status arm since r3g, in their row below; tr and wc join the vocabulary cross-check). No change for the r5b text either: the two listing reads whose status is kept apart as listing_rc (819, 876) are listed as bare R rows with no s tag, since the s pattern reads `|| rc=$?` alone, their arms on the next lines (820, 877) as bare R rows the p pattern does not match (it reads `[ "$rc" -eq 0 ] || unscanned` alone), so both are judged from their text; the two counts (847, 1342) are listed as j rows by their grep; the size read (1125), the renames listing (1217) with its closer (1221) and the two previous-version reads (1225, 1231) are plain git statements with their status arms; the short file's cat (1083) a suppressed substitution with no arm, judged safe below; the rev-list --parents test inside the previous-version condition (the r3g text's 1013) is gone, the count read from the caller's pcount file by an awk command 1 does not list; the added lines' capture (1389) is a plain status-read helper call, its judge grep (1391) reading the file; the population awks (853, 1020) and the pcount read (1212) run over the hook's own scratch files and are reads of no object (command 2 lists them as substitutions and truncating filters, in their rows below). No change for the r6b text either, though the tags read differently on it: a judged_read call is listed through the git, grep or helper name after its `--` on the same line (39 of the 53 call lines; the helper's own name is in neither alternation, so the 14 call lines whose command is tr, wc, sed, awk or one of the joins are command 2's alone), tagged a where the call ends in `|| :` (the a pattern reads `|| :` as a suppression; the status is captured in read_rc inside the helper and judged against the call's expected set, so the closure is at the helper and not in the caller's shape) and d where the call is an if head; the three numstat_rows calls (1227, 1228, 1248) are listed for the git their clauses name, R rows the s pattern does not match; the seven report reads command 1 lists (the eighth, the pcount awk, is command 2's alone) and the scanner's exit keep their `|| rc=$?` shapes and are listed as suppressed status reads as before, each marked `outside judged_read` at its site; the two configuration reads and the stdin read are listed as before. The tags are a first cut, not the verdict: a helper's pipeline shows c at the helper and its closure shows s at the caller, and since r6 a read through judged_read shows a or d at the call and its closure inside the helper. No change for the r7b text either: the five reads new in r7 are judged_read call lines listed through their grep or their git (1248 and 1249 the tip's two counts, j rows; 2169 the tag object and 2172 its size, R rows; 1735 and 2037 the two greps, retagged, j rows) or command 2's alone (2015, the added lines' wc), and the chosen-addresses read (569) is listed through its git; the ref list's shell loop (465 to 467) is in neither alternation (its `refs="$(cat)"` was a b-tagged R row until r7), so the stdin read is command 2's L row alone; added_lines' pipeline (540) is a plain R row, its `| ROMP_COUNT_FILE=$3 awk` no longer meeting the c pattern's `| awk`; the tag's shell parse (2185 to 2196) is command 2's alone, its closer 2195 an i row of command 1; tag_field's and tag_message's pipelines (525 and 530 at r6b) are gone with the functions; the two message_hit_lines calls (2126, 2212) are listed by neither command since r7, the clause they pass no longer naming grep (the subject alone); the marker arm of the message log (2124) is an R row the p pattern does not match, the read-list arm's line and the object field's line (1214, 2223) p rows now, beginning with unscanned.

```bash
awk '
{ line = $0
  if (buf != "") line = buf " " line
  if (line ~ /\\$/) { sub(/[ \t]*\\$/, "", line); buf = line; if (!start) start = NR; next }
  n = start ? start : NR; start = 0; buf = ""
  code = line; sub(/^[ \t]*#.*$/, "", code); sub(/[ \t]#[ \t].*$/, "", code)
  reads = "(^|[^A-Za-z0-9_$.-])(git|cat|command -v|\"\\$gl\"|rev_range|added_lines|chosen_emails|is_chosen|stamped_addresses|tag_field|tag_message|parent_count|scannable_commits)([ \t)]|$)"
  judge = "(^|[^A-Za-z0-9_$.-])grep([ \t)]|$)"
  if (code !~ reads && code !~ judge && code !~ /\[ (! )?-[fx] / && code !~ /done < /) next
  shape = ""
  if (code ~ /2>\/dev\/null|>\/dev\/null 2>&1|\|\| *true|\|\| *:( |$)|\|\| *\{ *[a-z_]+=""/) shape = shape "a:suppressed "
  if (code ~ /=\$\(|="\$\(/ && code !~ /\|\|/ && code !~ /^[ \t]*(if|while|elif) /) shape = shape "b:subst-no-arm "
  if (code ~ /(git|cat|added_lines|stamped_addresses|tag_field|tag_message|chosen_emails|parent_count|scannable_commits)[^|]*\| *(grep|awk|while|cut|sed|sort|tr)/) shape = shape "c:pipe-left "
  if (code ~ /^[ \t]*(if|while|elif|case) / || code ~ /\[ "\$\(/ || code ~ /\) *&& *\[/) shape = shape "d:test "
  if (code ~ /\|\| *rc=\$\?|\|\| *\{ *rc=\$\?|\|\| *return \$\?|\|\| *\{ *[a-z_]*=?"?"?;? *(unscanned|tag_unread|type_unread)|\|\| *\{ *$/) shape = shape "s:status-read "
  if (code ~ reads) shape = shape "R "
  if (code ~ judge && code !~ /git grep/) shape = shape "j "
  if (code ~ /\[ (! )?-[fx] /) shape = shape "f:file-test "
  if (code ~ /done < /) shape = shape "i:input-redirect "
  if (code ~ /^[ \t]*(echo|unscanned) / || code ~ /^[ \t]*\[ "\$rc" -eq 0 \] \|\| unscanned/) shape = shape "p:report-text "
  gsub(/^[ \t]+/, "", line)
  printf "%d\t%s\t%s\n", n, shape, line }
' .githooks/pre-push
```

For an older text replace the file argument with `<(git show <sha>:.githooks/pre-push)`. Command 2 is new in r1, for the WIDENED class: a read that succeeds and hands on LESS than its source holds, with no status to read. One row per statement that can lose part of its input or turn "less" into "absent": a `read` loop with what feeds it (L, with file, herestring or pipe: a final line with no newline comes back from `read` with status 1 and ends a plain `while read` before the body runs; a herestring always ends in a newline, a file or a pipe may not), a command substitution (S: strips every trailing newline and every NUL byte), a parameter expansion that cuts the value (P), a truncating filter (T: head, tail, -n, --max-count, an awk exit, sed -n, cut, sort -u, uniq, tr, grep -v, grep .), a read option that reads less than the object by design (N: -I, -l, -M, -c, --not, --remotes, --diff-merges, -U0, --no-show-signature, --format), a test that treats an empty value as absent (E). The hook's only readers of its own captured text are read, `$(...)`, the parameter expansions and the filters, so listing every statement that has one lists every site of the class; the command cannot see whether a given strip LOSES anything, which is what the table judges. Known false positives, left so the listing is reproducible: L on report lines whose text contains "read " and T on the top-level `exit 1`. Command 1 lists a read loop's CLOSER (`done <`) and command 2 its HEAD (`while read`): the head is the row the f5 method could not see, and the denylist reader's head is where the f5 entry was wrong.

```bash
awk '
{ line = $0
  if (buf != "") line = buf " " line
  if (line ~ /\\$/) { sub(/[ \t]*\\$/, "", line); buf = line; if (!start) start = NR; next }
  n = start ? start : NR; start = 0; buf = ""
  code = line; sub(/^[ \t]*#.*$/, "", code); sub(/[ \t]#[ \t].*$/, "", code)
  if (code ~ /^[ \t]*$/) next
  t = ""
  if (code ~ /(^|[ \t;])read[ \t]/ || code ~ /done <|\| *while/) {
      t = t "L:read-loop "
      if (code ~ /done < /) t = t "L:file "
      if (code ~ /done <<< /) t = t "L:herestring "
      if (code ~ /\| *while/) t = t "L:pipe "
  }
  if (code ~ /\$\(/) t = t "S:subst "
  if (code ~ /\$\{[A-Za-z_!#@*0-9]+(%%|%|##|#|:[0-9])/ || code ~ /^[ \t]*case /) t = t "P:param-trim "
  if (code ~ /(^|[ \t|(])(head|tail|uniq|cut|tr|sort -u|sed -n)([ \t]|$)/ || code ~ /--max-count|-n 1( |$)| -1 | exit[ ;}]/ || code ~ /grep -[a-zA-Z]*v|grep \. /) t = t "T:truncate "
  if (code ~ / -I | -l | -M | -c |--not|--remotes|--diff-merges|-U0|--first-parent|--no-show-signature|--format/) t = t "N:narrow "
  if (code ~ /\[ -[nz] |\$\{#|(^|[ \t;&|])(continue|break)([ \t;]|$)|\|\| *return 0/) t = t "E:empty-test "
  if (t == "") next
  gsub(/^[ \t]+/, "", line)
  printf "%d\t%s\t%s\n", n, t, line }
' .githooks/pre-push
```

Its result on the r1 text, before the repair and after it: 98 rows (17 L, of which 1 reads a file and 5 read herestrings; 33 S; 31 P; 12 T; 7 N; 25 E); on the f5 text 83 (15; 1, 5; 28; 26; 10; 6; 20); on the r2 text 106 (21 L, of which 1 reads a file and 6 read herestrings; 36 S; 31 P; 14 T; 7 N; 26 E): the eight new rows are refuse_replace_refs' five (its listing, S and N; its absent test, E; its loop head and herestring closer, L; its report line, an L false positive on the word read), the object derivation's two pipelines (S; the second also T for its `sort -u` over blob shas, which loses nothing since the sizes are keyed by sha) and its awk's `exit 3` (T, the fail-closed exit on a missing blob), and the `--log-opts` string in gitleaks_args (N, the nine plain-stream options by design) in place of the r1 invocation's N tag; the count clause at 660 is a second new L false positive (the words `a git read exited`); on the r2c text 150 (34 L, of which 5 read files and 6 read herestrings; 44 S; 49 P; 15 T; 9 N; 39 E): 47 rows are new by their text and three r2 rows are gone in the same move (the denylist file test, the opt-out and the executable test, restated as identifier_scan_armed's file test at 342, the opt-out inside the predicate's arm at 802 and gitleaks_binary's test at 347), 44 net; eighteen P, the record trims, the case labels and the report lines' sha cuts; three S, the mktemp at 401, the driver key read at 451 and the blob read at 462, the last also T for `head -c 8000` and N for that same flag, a false positive of the ` -c ` pattern; E), gitleaks_args' `--log-opts` string at 828 (`--root` its tenth option; N) and the showRoot arm's test at 919 (the variable in place of its own config substitution; S for its rev-list substitution, E), the other 150 rows identical by their text; Since round r2f the assessment is pinned by execution: bats case 173 (r2f's 88) makes this read fail (a git shim exiting 128 on the log.showRoot read alone) beside a scanner wrapper that drops --root, under the key false with a root credential, and the push is still REFUSED on the count without the key line; a mutant that let the failed read return from scan_credentials turns the case red.

At r2 the object derivation counted the root's entries while the key hid the root's diff from gitleaks' log, so the push was refused `covered N-1 of the N commits with content to scan` where the r1 text had published a credential in the root commit (bats case 65, red at the r1 text), on the premise that no option turns the root's diff back on; r2c added the line naming the key and the remedy, the ruling's condition on an accepted fail-closed case (case 65 asserted it, red at the r2 text; 66 the control). Round r2e found the premise FALSE by execution (`git log -p --root` under the key set false prints the root commit's hunk: two hunks with the option, one without, git 2.43.0, both gitleaks versions), implemented the environment override romp-manager's ruling of 2026-09-21 15:52Z had taken on that premise (a GIT_CONFIG_COUNT pair setting the key true for the hook's child gits) and flagged the option; round r2h, the fold owner's call (rulings-r2h.md, flagged for romp-manager's confirmation in round 3, the pair recoverable from the r2e fixer log), replaced the pair with `--root` in gitleaks_args' `--log-opts`, the shape `--pretty` and `--date` take: the option outranks the key in every scope and travels on the scanner's command line, so it reaches a scanner git that does not inherit the hook's environment, the very case the pair's arm existed for, and the hook exports nothing beyond the replacement switch. Decided by execution at both texts (the r2h fixer and auditor, real pushes through the installed hook with the count unset): a clean root push under the key false, refused on the count at the r2c text, passes with `2 commits scanned`; a root credential under it, refused on the count there, is FOUND as a finding; a scanner wrapper stripping every GIT_CONFIG pair scans the root (the option travels); a wrapper removing the word `--root` from the log options and keeping every other is refused on the count with the re-aimed line (the one way the arm fires for this key now); a caller's own pair is seen alone in the scanner's git's command scope, read through a git shim in the exec path, and a caller's pair setting the key FALSE at index 0 still passes with both commits scanned. Bats cases 65 (re-aimed: the credential FOUND, the road shown first by scan_direct_without_root, the hook's own list less the option), 66 (a clean root passes), 67 (the negative pin: the hook exports no pair), 68 (the environment-stripping wrapper passes), 69 (the option-stripping wrapper, refused with the line) and 70 (the control: a shortfall under the key unset names no key; r2c's 66): 65 to 69 red at the r2c text by their property (65 and 66 first at scan_direct_without_root's pin that the hook's list carries the option, 65 also at the finding; 67 and 68 at the pass; 69 at the re-aimed line), 70 green under both texts.
