THE COLOUR ROAD, the ninth, of the widened class, on two statements of the r1 text (523 scannable_commits and 598 the gitleaks invocation; on the r2 text the derivation at 569-583 and the invocation at 674, on the r2c text 749-763 and 853, on the r2e text 778-792 and 885), FIXED in r1 by the round's audit and, as the first found instance of the class the next paragraph states, closed structurally in r2; no OPEN row remains on the committed text. The fault: a stream that holds less than the objects, read alike by both readers, so the "independent" count agrees with the emptied scan. With `color.ui=always` or `color.diff=always` in any config scope git reads (the clone's, or a user's global gitconfig, which reaches gitleaks' git the same way), git colours `log -p` written to a pipe. gitleaks 8.30.1 and 8.28.0 find no file header in the SGR-prefixed stream, report `0 commits scanned` and `no leaks found`, write no ERR line (git wrote nothing to stderr) and exit 0; the fixer's scannable_commits read the same coloured stream and its `^diff --git ` and `^@@` patterns missed the same lines, so expected 0 equalled reported 0, both conditions passed, and the hook exited 0. By execution: the control refuses as a finding on every text; with either key set, a REAL push through the installed hook, the denylist armed, published a branch carrying two live credentials assembled at run time with nothing printed but gitleaks' two INF lines, at the f5 text and at the fixer's r1 text alike. gitleaks scanning nothing under colour is pre-existing (every text before r1 passes there too); what r1's first text added was a count condition derived from the same `git log -p` stream, which therefore shared every upstream transform with the scan it checked: a count derived from the same stream is independent of the scanner's PARSING, not of the stream's TRANSFORMS. The fix, the smallest edit on both statements: `git log --no-color -p -U0 ...` in scannable_commits, so the derivation reads a stream it has cleaned itself, and `--log-opts="$range --diff-merges=first-parent --no-color"` on the gitleaks invocation, so the scanner's git is given the same (an option on the log outranks color.diff=always in every scope). The first alone was tried and is not enough: the secrets push is refused `covered 0 of the 2` (fail-closed) but a CLEAN push under colour is refused too (`covered 0 of the 1`: gitleaks still scans nothing), so both reads get the option. The `--log-opts` form rather than a `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` prefix on the invocation (also verified to work): tests/git-hermetic.bash exports `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT=5` with five pairs for every bats run, and a prefix of 2 would override them for gitleaks' git. Comments at the three sites say why: one sentence in the hook's header paragraph, a paragraph at the end of scannable_commits' comment, two lines in the invocation's comment. Two bats cases at the end of the gitleaks section of tests/pre-push-hook.bats: a credential under `color.ui=always` is refused as a FINDING (the coloured stream shown by execution first, then the hook: status 1, `2 commits scanned`, the github-pat rule named, `gitleaks found a credential`, and not the incomplete-scan line), and a clean push under `color.diff=always` passes with `1 commits scanned` and no hook line, so the counts agree for the right reason. Fails-before: against the f5 text the file runs 1..50 with 41 ok and 9 not ok (the fixer's seven, plus 49 at `[ "$status" -eq 1 ]`, the fail-open, and 50 at `1 commits scanned`, since gitleaks reported 0 there); against the fixer's r1 text 48 ok and 2 not ok (49 and 50 at the same assertions). So case 50 is not a bare pass control: it pins that gitleaks reads the commit under colour, the scanner-side half of the repair. The real-push probe with the committed hook, gitleaks 8.30.1 and 8.28.0: the control refused as a finding; the noise mode refused on both clauses; the short mode by the count alone (`1 of the 2`); the fail mode on both; the rename warning refused as a finding on the secrets branch and passing on the clean one; a clean push exit 0 with 0 hook lines; under `color.ui=always` and under `color.diff=always` the secrets branch refused as a FINDING (`2 commits scanned`, `leaks found: 2`) and the clean push passing (`1 commits scanned`); the remote holding main alone after every run. The identifier arm is unaffected (its tip grep has `--no-color` since r1, diff-tree since f5, and ls-tree, cat-file, rev-list, config and `log --format` print no colour, as check-attr -z and `cat-file blob` since r2c, and the read-list grep with `--no-color`, the numstats with -z and a merge's combined patch with `--no-color`, the same read as added_lines, since r3 to r3d; all five hit kinds named under `color.ui=always`). Beside it, the widened method surfaced boundaries that need a deliberate configuration, recorded at r1 for a follow-up and not counted as roads; review round r2 found by execution that they were instances of the class and closed all but half of one: B1 a content-hiding textconv that succeeds silently (at r1 gitleaks scanned the converted, empty content and the derivation counted the same commit, and a REAL push published a credential at the r1 text; CLOSED in r2 by the object derivation, with `--no-textconv` in gitleaks_args so a clean push agrees and the dirty push is refused as a finding); B2 a `-diff` or `binary` attribute on a text file, in two halves: the credential half (gitleaks' `log -p` printed no hunk for the file, and a real push published at the r1 text) CLOSED in r2 by `--text`, under which gitleaks scans what git would call binary and the derivation counts every changed non-empty blob, binary or not; the identifier half OPEN on the r2 text as a documented boundary: `git grep -I` takes the attribute's binary as binary and `diff-tree -p` prints no added line for such a file, so a banned string in a text file under a -diff attribute published through the identifier arm at the r2 text (shown by the round's auditor with a real push, the denylist armed and gitleaks off), the documented binary boundary widened from content to attribute; not closed by a `--text` on the identifier arm, which would change a documented contract (a binary file's string is a pass), and ruled IN-FOLD and CLOSED in r2c by REFUSAL rather than by scanning, the shape refuse_replace_refs set, refuse_hidden_paths at r2c reading the diff attribute of every blob the two content reads would read; that closure was keyed on the attribute's VALUE, and round r3 found it FALSE by execution (a text blob over core.bigFileThreshold in a middle commit and one under a driver named a reserved word each published through a real push at 89589e7d3, the size key being a way git calls a blob binary that no attribute names), so since r3 the half is closed by git's OWN VERDICT on each read: the tip's grep asked what it read, and the per-commit diff's numstat over the pair it reads, or a merge's own combined patch, asked what it printed no hunk for, and a blob so skipped whose bytes are text is refused rather than read as empty, whatever rule made git call it binary, so the contract stands (a blob binary by its bytes passes under any attribute or key, contract shape c6) and hidden text is never read as empty; per-commit ONLY, since the size key does not reach the grep and a tip-side size rule would be a false refusal, and an explicit `diff` attribute outranks the key in every read and prints the hunk, so it is a hit and never a refusal (the paragraph after the class paragraph); B3 a replace ref (every read of both arms followed the replacement while `git push` transferred the ORIGINAL object: a real push published the banned string and the credential at the pushed sha; a third shape beside "fails" and "less": a read that returns OTHER than the pushed object) CLOSED in r2 by `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1` exported for every git the hook runs and a refusal before either scan under any replace ref (refuse_replace_refs); B4 the over-refusal under `format.pretty` set to a non-default format (oneline, raw, email among them: gitleaks parses the default header, attributes no commit, and a CLEAN push was refused `covered 0 of the 1`) CLOSED in r2 by `--pretty=medium` in gitleaks_args, with `--date=default` beside it for the same over-refusal under `log.date` set to relative or human, found in r2; B5 `log.showSignature=true` with a signed commit git cannot verify, labelled at r1 an over-refusal by assessment and shown by execution in r2 to be worse (on the refuters' runner the f5 text PUBLISHED a credential under it with `0 commits scanned` and the r1 text refused clean pushes; on the fold's own box gpg's report went into the log's stdout and gitleaks parsed past it) CLOSED in r2 by `--no-show-signature` in gitleaks_args, which the hook's two other log reads already carried. No boundary of the five stays open on the r2c text, nor on the r2e text, where the r2c flags were taken as ruled (the paragraph after the class paragraph). On the r2e text the identifier half was open again by the enumeration's gap, found and closed by derivation in round r3; rounds r3c to r3f closed the roads and the false refusals the derivation's first scope left (a symlink's target over the size key; a merge's symlink under an attribute; an empty file a merge adds; a merge's third-path move), so no boundary of the five stays open on the r3f text.

THE CLASS BEHIND THE COLOUR ROAD, transforms of a stream both readers shared, found by review round r2 and closed structurally in the same round (2026-09-21). The r1 fix gave the hook two coverage conditions so one failing could not hide the other, and its own audit found they were not independent under colour and patched that with `--no-color`. Round r2 found five more transforms of the same `git log -p` stream on the committed r1 text, each by execution: a `.gitattributes` `diff=<driver>` entry with a hiding `diff.<driver>.textconv`, and a `-diff` attribute on the file carrying the credential, each rewrote or emptied the patch for gitleaks AND for the hook's count, so both conditions passed and a REAL push through the installed hook published a live credential assembled at run time with zero hook lines; `format.pretty` set to a non-default format and `log.date` set to relative each broke gitleaks' header parse while its content coverage stayed whole, so a CLEAN push was refused `covered 0 of the 1` with advice pointing at disabling the scan; and `log.showSignature=true` with an unverifiable signature wrote gpg's report into the stream (the refuters' runner: the f5 text published, the r1 text refused clean pushes). Six transforms in two rounds, three of which published a credential while the hook reported clean and three of which refused clean pushes, from one mechanism: a count derived from the stream it checks is only as independent as the stream is clean, and patching transforms one at a time keeps finding roads (romp-manager's ruling for the round, section A: a wrong mechanism, not a missing case). The fix takes the count off the stream: scannable_commits reads the OBJECTS, `git rev-list --parents` over the range (every commit gitleaks' log walks, each with its first parent), one `git diff-tree --stdin -r --raw --no-renames --root` over those pairs (each commit's changed paths against its first parent, a root against the empty tree, with their blob shas, applying no attribute, driver or colour) and one `git cat-file --batch-check` over the added and type-changed blobs; a commit has content when one entry that is not a deletion gives the patch a hunk: a gitlink whose sha changed (a `Subproject commit` line either way), a modified blob whose sha changed (a mode-only change keeps the sha and prints no hunk; a file emptied prints one), or a new or type-changed path whose blob is not empty (an empty new file or link prints no hunk); a blob cat-file cannot find exits 3 with a fatal line and refuses, and any of the three reads failing refuses at the caller (`a git read exited N`), never counting 0. The plain-stream options are hardening, not the fix: gitleaks_args builds gitleaks' argument list in ONE place, which the hook's invocation and tests/pre-push-hook.bats's scan_direct (which evals the function out of the hook file) both call, so the two match by construction; its `--log-opts` value carries the range, `--diff-merges=first-parent` and nine options at r2, ten since r2h, each outranking the config key it neutralises in every scope: `--no-color`, `--no-textconv`, `--text` (a -diff attribute or core.bigFileThreshold makes git call a file binary and print no hunk for it; under the option gitleaks scans what git would call binary, and the derivation counts every changed non-empty blob, binary or not), `--no-renames` (a pure rename is a deletion and an addition for both reads), `--submodule=short` and `--ignore-submodules=none` (diff.submodule and diff.ignoreSubmodules otherwise drop a gitlink hunk from gitleaks' stream and refuse a clean push), `--no-show-signature`, `--pretty=medium` and `--date=default`, and since r2h `--root` (log.showRoot, below); three of the nine r2 options (`--no-renames` and the two submodule options) are the round's fixer's additions beyond the ruling's list, each a false-refusal remedy recorded in the round's log with its alternative. Verified by execution: the hook's own scannable_commits and gitleaks_args, read out of the hook file, against gitleaks 8.30.1 and 8.28.0 over 40 commit shapes (round r1's 22, with the binary-only and rename-only commits now counted by all three; eleven typechange and gitlink shapes; files with and without a trailing newline; a replace ref with and without the export) and over the fork's own HEAD~300..HEAD range at 3a05b63bb, the round 2 commit's parent and the round 2 fixer's staged-tree base (300 first-parent steps; 4513 commits by rev-list; 4502 with content on all three counts, the derivation and both versions; the figures derived here once and referred to from the count read's row above and from the body: re-derived in round r6c from the committed hook's scannable_commits over 3a05b63bb~300..3a05b63bb, `git rev-list --count` 4513, `--first-parent` 300, the derivation 4502 in 0.8 s, and gitleaks 8.30.1 and 8.28.0 each reporting `4502 commits scanned` and `no leaks found` under gitleaks_args' log options, in 59 s each), 0 mismatches; 36 diff- and log-affecting config keys swept, every one passing through under the final option set (two, diff.submodule and diff.ignoreSubmodules, needed the two submodule options); the derivation takes 0.8 s over that range where the r1 patch read took 44 s, and gitleaks itself 55 s under `--text --no-renames` where it took 45 s, a push faster overall. `log.showRoot` set to false, which hides a root commit's diff from gitleaks' log while the derivation counts the root's entries, was named at r2 and r2c as a fail-closed over-refusal rather than a road (a push carrying a root commit refused `covered N-1 of the N commits with content to scan`, the r1 text having published a credential in a root commit under the key; since r2c one line after the coverage line naming the key and the remedy) on the premise that no option turns the root's diff back on. The premise was false: round r2e found by execution that `--root` outranks the key (`git log -p --root` under the key set false prints the root's hunk, two hunks with the option and one without, under git 2.43.0 and both gitleaks versions), so romp-manager's ruling of 2026-09-21 15:52Z, which on that premise had reversed the refusal to an environment override (a GIT_CONFIG_COUNT pair setting the key true for the hook's child gits, staged in r2e with a refusal arm for a scanner whose git does not inherit it), was replaced in round r2h by the fold owner with the option itself (rulings-r2h.md, flagged for romp-manager's confirmation in round 3; the pair is recoverable from the r2e fixer log). NEUTRALISED: `--root` is the tenth option in gitleaks_args' `--log-opts`, the shape `--pretty` and `--date` take, and it outranks the key in every scope and travels on the scanner's command line, so a root commit's diff is scanned (its credential found as a finding), a clean root push passes with the scanner's count the hook's own, and a scanner git that does not inherit the hook's environment still reads it. REFUSES: a scanner whose git does not honour the option, or any other count that comes up short, is refused on the count as before, and when the short count meets the key false in the clone's configuration (showroot_configured, the file value read once at the top of the hook) and a root commit in the range, the refusal also names the key, says the scanner's git did not honour the `--root` option the hook passes on its log, and gives the remedy (set the key to true or unset it and push again, or `ROMP_NO_GITLEAKS=1` for that one push). The ruling's reason (romp-manager's, restated by the fold owner for the option): fail-closed is the fallback for what the hook cannot see, not the answer when it can neutralise the cause; a refusal a contributor must fix by editing their own git config, for something the hook could have ignored, is a false refusal; the key belongs in the hardening list beside format.pretty and log.date; and the option is the narrowest mechanism, one word on one command line, where the pair injected configuration into every git the hook runs. Beyond the replacement switch the hook exports nothing for the gits it runs (a caller's GIT_CONFIG pairs pass through untouched, a negative pin). The two halves of the hook differ in KIND, stated at its header: the credential half's own count is derived from the objects and no configuration touches it; the identifier half reads a log and diff stream (as the scanner inside the credential half does), so it is config-dependent by construction. What can hide a blob from its two content reads is closed since r3 by a DERIVED PROPERTY rather than key by key: git's own verdict on each read against the blob's bytes (refuse_hidden_paths: git produced no hunk for a blob that is text), which covers the diff attribute, core.bigFileThreshold, a driver under any name and a rule a later git adds with one mechanism and no enumeration to keep current, in a one-parent commit's diff and in a merge's combined patch alike. That sentence is what keeps the next key from reopening this entry, and the enumeration that failed is why it is the sentence: at r2c the half was hardened by an enumeration of attribute values (round r2c's attribute road closed by `unset`, a binary-true driver and the two reserved words that do not hide), and round r3 found the size key and check-attr's reserved words outside it by real pushes, the same class round r2 had ruled on for the credential half (a guard keyed on a name, a spelling or a value rather than on the property), so the entry keys on the effect and not on the cause. The scanner's log is hardened key by key as keys are found (log.showRoot, by `--root` on the scanner's log, in r2e and r2h; another key another day), and so are the identifier half's other reads (colour on its grep, the signature report on its log, each by the option that outranks the key); reading the identifier half's input from the objects is the long fix, named and not taken in this fold. Beside the class, the premise the whole hook rests on is stated at the hook's header and enforced: both scans read the objects at the pushed shas on the premise that what they read is what the push transfers; a replace ref breaks it (every read followed the substitute while the push transferred the original, and a real push published a banned string and a credential at the pushed sha with both scans reading clean), so `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1` is exported beside `set -euo pipefail` for every git the hook runs, gitleaks' own included, and refuse_replace_refs, run before either scan, refuses the push under any `refs/replace/` ref (or under the namespace `GIT_REPLACE_REF_BASE` names), naming each ref and the premise: a scan under replacement cannot vouch for the bytes pushed. At the r2 text the refusal was unconditional (a clone with no denylist and no gitleaks was refused too when it carried one), the ruling's text taken and the alternative (refuse only when a scan is armed) recorded in the round's log; round r2c ruled for the alternative and NARROWED the gate: refuse_replace_refs lists the replace refs only when identifier_scan_armed (the denylist file exists, at ROMP_PRIVATE_STRINGS or the XDG default, through private_strings_file) or credential_scan_armed (ROMP_NO_GITLEAKS unset and a gitleaks binary resolving through ROMP_GITLEAKS or PATH and executable, through gitleaks_binary) holds, the two predicates scan_identifiers' and scan_credentials' own early returns read, so the gate and the scans agree by construction, and the predicates decide only whether a scan runs (an empty denylist arms a scan that greps for nothing; a skipped or absent scanner keeps its silence or its notice). The reason, the ruling's: the refusal prevents a false CLEAN REPORT, and where no scan runs there is no report and nothing false to prevent, so a clone that never asked for either scan stays unaffected, as CLAUDE.md promises (the identifier scan a no-op on an absent denylist; gitleaks absent a loud notice and no scan). By execution through real pushes at the r2c text: a replace ref with neither scan armed passes with no replace line (the not-installed notice printing where the binary is absent and the skip not asked), and each scan armed alone is refused on the premise; at the r2 text the first two were refused. CLAUDE.md's gitleaks bullet states the refusal class for a contributor refused by it: a gitleaks that ran but cannot show what it scanned (an error line in its own log, a count that is not the hook's own, no count at all) refuses the push, and since r2h says the hook passes `--root` on the scanner's log so a root commit's diff is scanned whatever `log.showRoot` says, and that a scanner whose git does not honour it refuses on the count and says so, naming the key and the remedy. In tests/pre-push-hook.bats (207 cases since r7b, 202 at r7, 193 at r6b, 185 at r6, 173 at r5b, 167 at r5, 150 at r3g, 146 at r3f, 139 at r3e, 134 at r3d, 123 at r3c, 111 at r3 after its audit's one repair, 85 since r2e and r2h, 80 at r2c, 65 at r2, 50 at r1): the two refusal arms round r1 left untested are pinned (case 51, a stub scanner exiting 0 with no count line: `reported no commit count`; case 52, the hook's own count read refused alone by a `fail_diff_tree_stdin` wrapper over `git_refusing`: `could not be counted for the credential scan (a git read exited 128)`), each red when its own arm is deleted from the hook and green when the other's is; the shapes control (case 45) is re-cut for the resolved invocation (seven commits, three counted: a binary add and a pure rename count as their bytes say; an empty commit, a deletion, a mode-only change and an empty new file for nothing); scan_direct reads gitleaks_args out of the hook (a hook that renames the function fails the helper loudly rather than drifting from it); and thirteen transform cases (53 to 65, with the helpers hiding_textconv, signed_commit and substitute_for): one dirty push per transform refused as a FINDING (the textconv, the -diff attribute, format.pretty=oneline, log.date=relative, the signed commit under log.showSignature=true) and one clean push per setting passing with the scanner's own count and no hook line, the replace ref refused on the premise line with the finding beside it (dirty) and on the premise alone (clean), and log.showRoot=false refused on the count alone (65 at r2 and r2c; re-aimed in r2e and r2h to the credential FOUND, below). Fails-before, the new bats file against the r1 text (3a05b63bb), recorded per case and not as "each red before": of the 18 cases added or changed in r2, twelve are red at the r1 text by their property (45, 52, 53, 55, 56, 57, 58, 59, 60, 63, 64, 65: the textconv, -diff, replace-ref and showRoot dirty pushes published there, the format.pretty and log.date clean pushes were refused there, and the -diff clean control read `1 commits scanned` with the file skipped as binary), two red by construction only (46 and 47, whose scan_direct reads gitleaks_args out of a hook that has none), and four green at the r1 text (51, whose arm exists there and is pinned by the mutation; 54, the clean textconv control; 61 and 62, the signed commit, green on the fold's box, whose gpg writes its report where gitleaks parses past it, so those two pin the scanner-side option by reading the raw log for gpg). Round r2c added or changed 18 more, counted apart from r2's 18: fifteen new (66 to 80) and three changed (55 and 56, the credential half's two -diff cases, run with the denylist absent, since the armed identifier scan now refuses their pushes on the attribute before the property they pin is reached, the property unchanged and nothing retired; 65, which asserts the showRoot line): 66 the no-key control; 67 to 70 the gate under its four arming states (neither scan; the credential scan not skipped but no gitleaks on PATH, through path_without_gitleaks; the denylist alone; gitleaks alone); 71 to 78 the attribute road in eight forms, the first two through REAL pushes with the hook installed and the remote's state asserted (push_main_through_hook, remote_holds_main, attributes); 79 and 80 the check's own reads against their refusing inputs. Against the r2 text (b42231339), per case: ten red by their property (65, the key line absent; 67 and 68, refused with neither scan armed; 71, 72, 74, 76 and 77, the -diff text file published or passed through the identifier arm, in the tree, under a binary driver, in .git/info/attributes and in a middle commit; 79 and 80, reads the r2 text did not make) and eight green (55 and 56, the re-aimed pair; 66, no key line under either text; 69 and 70, refused under the unconditional gate too; 73, the binary blob's pass; 75, the textconv-only driver's hit; 78, the no-denylist no-op). Rounds r2e and r2h, one commit, added or changed six more, counted apart (r2e's own count at its index text, 84 cases: four new, 66, 67, 68 and 81, and 65 re-aimed to the credential FOUND under the pair, red before at 65 66 67 68 81; r2h then added one and re-aimed four from the pair to the option): five new (66 a clean root under log.showRoot=false passes; 67 the negative pin, the hook exports no GIT_CONFIG pair of its own, the caller's one pair seen alone in the scanner's git's command scope through gitleaks_git_recording_pairs; 68 the environment-stripping scanner wrapper, gitleaks_without_config_pairs, still scans the root, the option travelling on the command line; 69 the option-stripping wrapper, gitleaks_without_root_option, refused on the count with the arm line in full and the recorded option list the hook's own less that one word; 82 a pure rename of a hidden text file the remote already holds, through a real fixture push, refused with the rename clause and the explicit-diff remedy) and one re-aimed (65, the root commit's credential FOUND as a finding, its road shown first by scan_direct_without_root, the hook's own list less the option, with a pin that the list carries it); r2c's 66 to 80 are 70 to 85, 82 between them. Against the r2c text (e744c28d4), per case: 65, 66, 67, 68, 69 and 82 red by their property (65 and 66 first at scan_direct_without_root's pin, 65 also at the finding and the scanner's own count; 67 and 68 at the pass, refused on the count there; 69 at the re-aimed line; 82 at the explicit-diff remedy), 70 green under both texts; against the r2e index text (the pair), 65 to 69 red (67 at the command-scope equality, r2e's hook having appended its pair; 68 at the pass, refused there; 69 at the coverage line, the pair having found the credential where the option's absence should have refused), 70 and 82 green. Round r3 added or changed 25 (110 cases at its text, 111 after its audit's one repair, against 88 at 89589e7d3), counted apart: the count's two arms with the real scanner (46, 47; ruling B's tests-3), the showRoot arm's reworded line (71, 72 re-aimed under R4; 73 the shortfall from a non-key cause; round r3's extra7-2), the derivation case (74; ruling C), the delete-line gate (79; extra7-1), the pure-rename remedy and the changed rename (88, 89), the size-key cases (91 to 93), the drivers named set and unspecified with their control (94 to 96 at that text; 132 to 134 now), the arms' cases (97 to 107 at that text, with 101 the audit's repair for the changed-path listing; 135 to 138, 142 to 144, 164 and 168 to 170 now; ruling B's tests-2 and regression-3, each asserting status 1 AND the clause, since removing one refusal differs from removing the whole clause, which aborts under set -e with no explanation) and the file header's, the transform section's and the count-read case's comments (ruling C). Against 89589e7d3, per case, at the round's numbering: 19 red by their property (71, 73 and 110 at the reworded showRoot line; 74 at the key line's fact clause, the head's count refusing but printing the old line; 89 at the status, the changed rename from a -diff path PUBLISHED; 91 at the status, the size-key middle commit PUBLISHED through a real push; 93 at the commit-level line; 94 and 95 at the status, the drivers named set and unspecified PUBLISHED; 98, 99 and 104 at the renamed clauses; 100, 101, 102, 103 and 107 at the status, the head having no read-list grep, no numstat read, no newline guard and a batch check-attr that failed OPEN on an empty answer; 105 and 106 at the label clauses), and 101 red on its clause at the audit's numbering; green at that head by design 46, 47, 79, 88, 92, 96 and 97, each owing its red to a mutant (m10 and m11 the count's arms, m1 the for-each-ref arm, m13 the pure-rename rule; 79, 92 and 96 pin behaviour the rulings kept), and every one of the eighteen mutants red on its case (mutants.out), the nine refusal arms deleted together red on all eight arm cases. THE IDENTIFIER SCAN'S OWN INSTANCE OF THE CLASS, the identifier half of boundary B2, found open by the r2 audit by execution, closed in round r2c by refusal rather than by scanning, found OPEN AGAIN by review round r3 (2026-09-21) by execution, the r2c closure having been keyed on an enumeration, and closed since r3 by a DERIVED PROPERTY, git's own verdict on each read, its scope made equal to the property's by rounds r3c to r3f (2026-09-22). Both content reads of the identifier arm read what git calls TEXT: the tip's `git grep -I` skips a blob git calls binary and the per-commit `diff-tree -p` prints no line for one. At r2c the entry, the hook's header and CLAUDE.md enumerated how git calls a blob binary (its bytes, a NUL in the first 8000; or its PATH's diff attribute, a `-diff` line or the `binary` macro in .gitattributes, .git/info/attributes or the file core.attributesFile names, or a driver whose `diff.<driver>.binary` is true), and refuse_hidden_paths read check-attr's VALUE for every regular file at the tip and in each new commit (`unset` hides, a driver hides when its binary key reads true, `set` and `unspecified` do not), so a text file under such an attribute was refused rather than read, and a REAL push through the installed hook had published a synthetic denylist string in one at the r2 text. Round r3 found two ways past that enumeration, each reproduced by real pushes with the denylist armed and gitleaks reporting clean, independently by both refuters and again by the round's fixer: core.bigFileThreshold, a third way git calls a blob binary, in the diff and not in the grep (the hook's own credential half names the key and neutralises it with `--text`; the identifier half had not carried it across), so a text blob over the key printed `Binary files ... differ` in the per-commit read while check-attr answered `unspecified` and the check passed it; and a diff driver NAMED `set` or `unspecified`, with `diff.<driver>.binary` true, for which check-attr answers exactly that word, which the value arm read as no attribute. The ruling (rulings-r3, section A): judge by GIT'S OWN VERDICT, do not neutralise a key and do not parse a value; a `-c core.bigFileThreshold=<large>` on the per-commit read was rejected as a third per-key patch on a privacy surface after two rounds had found the same class, a `--text` on that read was rejected by execution (it reverses the documented binary-by-bytes pass), and the corrections a size-reading route needed (strictly greater than the effective threshold; the unset key falling back to git's built-in default and not to 0; its own cat-file read) were all corrections to reasoning about the cause, the signal to key on the effect; the finding's own fix for the reserved words (reading diff.<value>.binary for them) was rejected too, since it falsely refuses a clean push where `unspecified` is also the answer for a path with no attribute. THE MECHANISM as committed (rounds r3 to r3f, every read of it through judged_read since r6; refuse_hidden_paths, with the helpers as_lines, numstat_rows and combined_rows, and since r6 the joins as named functions the helper runs whole, tip_unlisted_read_file, tip_blobs, tip_candidates, merge_rename_candidates, unlisted_verdict_path, join_candidates and byte_counts, and since r7 nul_records, the shell's count of the rows a join appended): the per-commit half is judged by git's own verdict on the read the scan makes. The population is every non-deleted post-image whose object is a BLOB (a regular file or a symlink, modes 100644, 100755 and 120000; a gitlink names a commit), a type change judged as its new object (the diff prints it as a deletion and an addition judged alone: a numstat of the empty tree against the commit's tree for that path, the tree's name from git hash-object; pinned since r5 by cases 102 and 103, the new link GONE at the tip, where the r3g cases kept it at the tip and the tip-owns rule decided them), less a post-image whose blob equals every pre-image blob (a mode-only change: the diff prints its mode lines with no hunk and no Binary line, so it hid nothing new, while the pair's stat calls an unchanged blob over the key or holding a NUL binary; pinned since r5 by case 106, the change with the file GONE at the tip through a real push, since case 105 keeps the blob at the tip where the tip-owns rule decides it: the property is the blob's absence from the pushed tip, not the file's), and less a blob the pushed TIP's tree holds under any path, which the tip half judges (read by its grep or its symlink pass, or refused as hidden there), so the per-commit road covers only content ABSENT from the tip, the intermediate-commit shape, which bounds the exposure. For a commit with ONE parent, `git diff-tree --numstat` over the pair the per-commit diff reads (-M): a post-image git reports as binary, a dash for each count, is one the diff printed no hunk for, the same decision on the same two blobs (they agree on every blob with bytes; an empty blob under such a rule is the one pair the numstat calls binary while the diff prints its header alone, a candidate here that passes as empty); a PURE rename, which the diff prints nothing for, is judged as the addition of its new path by a second numstat (--no-renames), the rule the r2e rename refusal set, so the explicit diff line the advice names lifts it for the new path (cases 90 and 93: the remedy passes; a file under -diff renamed to a plain path AND changed in the same commit is refused, the diff having judged the pair by the path it came from and printed no hunk; the tip holds the renamed blob in case 90, so the tip-owns rule decides it there, and the exception itself is pinned since r5 by cases 91 and 92, the lifted rename with the new path GONE at the tip passing and its twin with no later diff line for the new path refused, red under m-rename-exception: the property pinned is the blob's absence from the pushed tip, not the file's). For a MERGE, the combined patch itself (`git diff-tree -p -r -M -c --root --no-commit-id --no-color`, the read added_lines makes, byte for byte), read again and parsed section by section (combined_rows, the header's path C-unquoted as git quotes it): a section printing `Binary files differ` is a path the patch printed no hunk for, a candidate; a section with a hunk was read; a path with the header alone is a candidate judged by its bytes (an added empty file passes as empty; a non-empty blob so printed would be refused as hidden, an arm no shape found reaches, since a header-only section for a blob with bytes is a mode change over content some parent holds); a path with no section whose blob some parent holds under a path the merge lacks is a pure rename, judged as the addition of its new path by the empty-tree numstat, the candidates read from the merge's deletions against EACH parent (`diff-tree --raw -m`, since the combined listing names a deletion only when every parent held the path); a path with no section that is no rename is a verdict the read owed and did not print, refused as unscanned. The combined patch judges by a rule of its own that no per-parent verdict reproduces: it applies no size rule, and it applies a path's diff attribute to a symlink as the pairwise diff does not, which is why r3d replaced the per-parent numstat approximation for merges (r3's disclosed excess, a merge's own change to a file over the key refused as hidden while the patch had printed its hunk, went with it). The tip half likewise derives the set git's grep treats as binary from git's own report: the same `git grep -I`, asked to list the non-empty files it READ (`-l -z -e ''`), the complement over the tree's regular files with bytes (`ls-tree -r -z -l`) being the files it skipped; a symlink's target is read whole by the symlink pass, by cat-file, whatever any attribute or key says. No attribute value is parsed anywhere for a decision: check-attr is read once per REFUSED path for the report's label alone (`diff.<value>.binary` looked up first for every answer, since a driver may be named `set`, `unspecified` or `unset`; then `unset` names the -diff line or macro; any other answer names no attribute, and the line states the blob's size and the clone's core.bigFileThreshold as facts a reader needs and not as a cause, or, for a one-parent commit's changed file whose previous version is binary by its bytes, that cause, the key's facts beside it where the key is set), and a check-attr that fails, answers about another path or answers nothing changes the words and never the verdict (since r5b the tail as well: a failed read says the attribute could not be read and that whether an attribute of its path accounts for the verdict is unknown, both facts kept and both remedies printed, where the r5 text asserted after a failed read what the read could not establish). Two SCOPE constraints, the ruling's, both by execution: per-commit ONLY, since `git grep -I` on the tip is unaffected by the size key and a tip-side size or binary rule would be a FALSE refusal (case 97: a big text file carrying the string AT the tip under the key is a HIT by the tip grep, which reads it, and the tip is never called hidden); and an explicit `diff` attribute keeps outranking the key, in the grep and in the diff alike, which still prints the hunk under the key, so it is a hit and never a refusal (case 96), and it is the remedy the advice names. Each blob so named is then read (`git cat-file blob`, the first 8000 bytes counted with od and awk, the rest drained) and judged by git's own byte rule with no diff machinery: no bytes (an empty blob hides nothing) or a NUL (binary, which the scan never read) passes as before, the documented pass (a binary file's string, contract shape c6, byte-identical on every text), and anything else is text the scan could not see: one line per place and path, `romp pre-push: <path> at the tip of <ref> (<sha10>) is text that its diff attribute (<unset, or diff=<driver>, binary>) hides from the identifier scan, so the push is refused rather than scanned`, or `<path> in commit <sha10> ...` for a new commit's post-image (the r2c lines, byte for byte), or, where no attribute accounts for the verdict, `... is text that git calls binary although its diff attribute reads <value>, so no attribute of its path accounts for the verdict (the blob is <n> bytes; core.bigFileThreshold is <value, or not set> in this clone's configuration); the identifier scan did not read it, so the push is refused rather than scanned` (a one-parent commit's symlink says instead that it is a symbolic link judged by its size and bytes and by no attribute of the path; a rename says the earlier path counted too; a changed file whose previous version holds a NUL says that version's bytes are the cause, and where the key is set adds the key's facts), hidden_paths=1 and failed_scan=1, and, after the ref loop, the advice: the two r2e lines naming the attribute as the cause with the explicit-diff remedy and the rename clause (byte for byte; since r5b printed only where a refused line named the attribute as the cause or could not read it, hidden_attr, where the r5 text printed them on every hidden refusal, the disclosed shape's included, so the first remedy the pusher read was for a cause every line said did not account for the verdict), since r3 a third line where a line named no attribute (hidden_unnamed: core.bigFileThreshold as the one key git has today, its value and the blob's size shown, raise it above the size or unset it and push again, and since r5b in its own words the explicit "<path> diff" line, which outranks the key as well for a regular file, since the attribute paragraph is no longer there to point at, while a one-parent commit's diff applies no attribute to a symbolic link's target, so for such a link the key is the remedy), and since r3e a fourth where a line named the previous version's bytes (hidden_prior: the hook scans only commits new to every fetched remote, so fetch the remote that holds the commit first and push again, or push from a clone that has; a new commit of the shape is refused the same way, since the scan cannot read its new text, and since r6b has a second line naming an explicit diff attribute on the PREVIOUS version's path, the old path for a rename and the file's own path otherwise, under which git treats that version as text whatever its bytes, so the diff prints the change and the scan reads it, named ahead of the bypass it is safer than; the hidden_attr paragraph stays withheld there, the gate's prior clause kept; extra5-4 and tests-1, cases 187 to 189, and 110, 113 and 114 asserting the line). Every read the check makes fails closed through unscanned naming the read (the scratch directory, the tip's listing and its read-list grep, each commit's listing, its two numstats, a merge's combined patch and its per-parent listing, the empty tree's name and the numstat against it, a cat-file, since r5 the byte judge's size read and since r5b the added lines' own scratch directory; since r6 each through judged_read with its expected set, the joins' awk and tr, combined_rows' awk and as_lines' tr and wc included, where a tool that could not run ended the hook by errexit with no line): the STATUS-KEYED class; and since r5 the EXIT-0 EMPTY-OR-SHORT-ANSWER class is gated as a class (round 4's ruling), each of the check's reads tagged gate= or own= at its call since r6 (round 5's ruling: the header's lists derived from the tags): a numstat or patch that exits 0 and answers for fewer paths than the commit changes is a SHORT READ refused as unscanned (ruling B's class, gated on every read having exited 0 so one cause prints and not two; since r5b naming the first path met no verdict), a commit's changed-path listing must name every path its numstats or its combined patch answer for (a listing that exits 0 with nothing, on a one-parent commit or on a merge, refused as listed short naming the listing and the path; the merge decided by the parent count the caller read, never by the listing's shape), the tip's -z listing must agree with the symlink pass's entry count and name every file the grep lists as read (refused as listed short or long naming both counts, or naming the file), each scratch listing's rewrite must hold as many bytes as its input with every count a run of digits (refused naming the rewrite and both answers), and a blob read as zero bytes passes as empty only when cat-file -s gives its size as 0 (refused naming the read and the size); a path holding a newline byte is refused as unscanned rather than judged by nothing; and whatever was listed is still judged. Decided by execution at the r3g text, each by a REAL push with a shim exiting 0 and answering nothing or short: the listing (cases 153, 154), the tip's listing (139, 140, 141), the rewrite (160 to 163) and the blob read (165, 166) each PUBLISHED a hidden text file or passed the push with nothing printed, and each is refused since r5; the reads whose empty answer is the object's own are the header's own= list, derived from the call sites and pinned by case 174 (the property paragraph above; the residual paragraph names the three joins among the ten since r7b; and since r7 the tip candidates join and the join of a commit's verdicts are gate=, the first against the -z listing's regular files with bytes less the grep's read list, the second against the count its awk recorded, each shown publishing by a real push with a silent awk and a silent tr at the r6b text, hook cases 195 to 198). The r2c flags ruled in r2e stand as recorded (a hidden text file at the tip and in a new commit named once per place, since r3d the tip's line alone where the tip holds the blob; an EMPTY blob passes; the pure rename refused on the safe side with the advice that earns it; the explicit `rm` by the exact minted path with the residual at the header). One shape is refused on purpose and DISCLOSED at the header with its remedy, not an open road: a commit that turns a file binary by its bytes into a TEXT file prints `Binary files differ` for the pair (either side binary makes the pair binary, whatever the key says), so the new text was read by neither check and the commit is refused as hidden, its line naming the previous version's bytes as the cause: since r5 for a one-parent commit's change or rename (the version in its parent, at the path the diff read it from) and for a merge whose result is such a text file while a parent's version is binary (the combined patch prints Binary for it too; that parent's version named), so every refusal of the class names its cause and the fetch remedy and no key advice, where until r5 the merge twin and the rename twin were refused with the two-fact line and the key advice, the cause unnamed (the r3e fixer's FLAG 1, ruled in round 4 as extra9-1 and extra9-2); three commits in this repository's own history have that shape (a source file that once held a raw NUL byte, replaced by a text version), a clone that has fetched any remote excludes them from every range, and a clone with NO remote-tracking refs pushing the history as a new ref is refused on them, so the remedy is to fetch first, or to push from a clone that has (found by the r3d fixer's whole-history run and confirmed exact by the r3e audit's, 2026-09-22; cases 110 to 114). Verified by execution at every text, each round's fixer and auditor apart: the r2c recipe still refused with the remote holding nothing; the r3 shapes by real pushes (16 of 16 at the r3 text, five PUBLISHED at 89589e7d3); the r3 audit's 36 refute rows; the r3c audit's 59-row matrix of every post-image kind under every rule tried, 59 of 59 as the property predicts; the r3d audit's 42 merge rows; the r3e and r3f audits' probes (the class paragraph above). THE SHAPES THAT PUBLISHED OR PASSED, named one by one because they are the argument that the identifier half needed closing in this fold and then closing again by derivation (romp-manager's condition; each verified by the round's fails-before and by a real push in a scratch clone; bats case numbers at the round's text, then at the committed text): (1) a text file under a `-diff` attribute in the tree's .gitattributes, carrying a denylist string, pushed with the denylist armed: PUBLISHED at b42231339 (the identifier scan's grep skipped it as binary and its added-lines diff printed no line); refused since e40a82da0, naming the path and the attribute, the remote holding nothing (case 71 at r2c, now 82). (2) The same file carrying no denylist string: PASSED unread at b42231339; refused since e40a82da0 on the premise alone, since the scan cannot see the text it would have to clear (case 72, now 83). (3) A text file under a named diff driver (`diff=hide`) whose `diff.hide.binary` is true in the clone's config, carrying the string: PUBLISHED at b42231339; refused since e40a82da0, the line naming the driver (case 74, now 85). (4) The `-diff` line placed in `.git/info/attributes` rather than in the tree, the file carrying the string: PUBLISHED at b42231339 (git's grep and diff read that file too); refused since e40a82da0 (case 76, now 87). (5) A text file under `-diff` carrying the string, added in a middle commit and deleted before the tip, the tip clean: PUBLISHED at b42231339 (the tip scan never saw it and the per-commit diff printed no line); refused since e40a82da0, the line naming the commit (case 77, now 88). (6) A text file over core.bigFileThreshold (the key set below its size in the clone's configuration) carrying the string, added in a middle commit and deleted before the tip, the tip clean, no attribute on its path: PUBLISHED at 89589e7d3 through a real push with the denylist armed and gitleaks reporting clean (the per-commit diff printed `Binary files ... differ` and no hunk, check-attr answered `unspecified`, and the r2c check passed it; the round r3 refuters' witness, reproduced by both, and the r3 fixer's real-push row k1); refused since 29bc29891, the line naming the commit, the blob's size and the key's value, the key advice line printed, the remote holding nothing (case 91 at r3, now 95). (7) A text file under a diff driver NAMED `set`, with `diff.set.binary` true, carrying the string, pushed with the denylist armed, and the same under a driver named `unspecified`: PUBLISHED at 89589e7d3 (check-attr answered the reserved word, which the value arm read as no attribute, and both content reads skipped the file; the r3 fixer's rows d1 and d2); refused since 29bc29891, the line naming the driver (`diff=set, binary`; `diff=unspecified, binary`), the remote holding nothing (cases 94 and 95 at r3, now 132 and 133), with the control the refuters asked for green at both texts: a driver merely named `unspecified`, with no attribute naming it on any path, hides nothing, and a clean push passes with nothing printed (case 96 at r3, now 134). Beside them, the two shapes the round r3 and r3c auditors found PUBLISHED at the derivation's first scope, each closed in the round that followed: (8) a SYMLINK whose target is over core.bigFileThreshold and carries the string, added in a middle commit and gone at the tip (the size key reaches a symlink's target blob in the per-commit diff, which prints `Binary files /dev/null and b/link differ` and no target, while no attribute does, and the tip's symlink pass reads every target by cat-file): PUBLISHED at 29bc29891 and at 89589e7d3 (the r3 audit's row s12: a real push with the key at 20 and a 124-byte target; the r3 population was regular files alone); refused since def7b95d1, the line naming the commit and the link (case 94 at r3c, now 98). (9) A MERGE's own symlink at a path under a `-diff` attribute, the string in its target, gone at the tip (the combined patch applies the attribute to a symlink and prints `Binary files differ`, where the pairwise diff, and so the per-parent numstat, print the target): PUBLISHED at def7b95d1 and at every earlier text (the r3c fixer's row m6, disclosed at that text as an open road with its witness, the deferred-fix condition met in one home of three until r3d); refused since f45ff1a11 through a real push, the line naming the merge, the link and its attribute (case 105 at r3d, now 115), and with it three roads of the same class the r3d audit found published at def7b95d1 (a merge's own symlink under the binary macro, an octopus merge's symlink under -diff, a merge changing a link's target under -diff), each refused since. Beside the nine, at the r2c text, two reads the r2 text did not make (r2c's 79 and 80, re-aimed in r3 to the label read and the cat-file, now 168 and 164), since r2e the pure rename (82, now 89), since r3 the arms' cases (135 to 138, 142 to 152 and 155 to 159) and the short-read gate (144), since r5 the second class's cases (139 to 141, 153, 154, 160 to 163 and 165 to 167) and the arms' pins off the tip (91, 92, 102, 103 and 106), since r5b the NUL-bearing added line (5, 6), the quoted paths (128, 129) and the octopus pair (130, 131); the contract runs byte-identical at every round (16 of 16; 17 of 18, the null-byte warning's line); the fork's own tip through the same reads, at 7899359a5, the head the r6b round checked it at (the counts are in the round records, since they move at every upkeep merge): none hidden, every regular file the grep skipped binary by its bytes (the tree's one .gitattributes marks a fixture `-text` only; no .git/info/attributes; core.attributesFile and core.bigFileThreshold unset; every diff attribute unspecified); the check's rows are 1164 to 1678 in the population above.

The method is the deliverable, not the count of roads: the population is what the two commands list PLUS execution at every site a command tags as a status read of an external binary (a read inside that binary is visible only as the binary's invocation, and only execution showed that its status does not carry the read), PLUS, since r1, the less-than-source question asked of every row (a read loop's head, a substitution's strip, a filter's cut, a stream transformed by the clone's own configuration before either reader sees it). The method's limits, for the next reader: the commands see statements, not reach (errexit closes a substitution only outside a condition context; the b tag excludes if and while heads, so each b row is judged by context); a helper's pipeline shows unclosed at the helper and closed at its caller; a count derived from the same stream as the scan is only as independent as the stream is clean, which is why r1 had both reads ask git for a plain stream, why the r1 derivation was run over the fixer's text before it was committed, and why r2 took the count off the stream altogether (a plain-stream option closes the transform it names; an object read closes the class), and why r2c, where the identifier arm's reads skip a blob on its path's attribute, refuses rather than adds a read (a scan that cannot see a blob cannot call it clean; reading it from the objects is the longer fix the round named and left); and why r2e re-ran a premise the entry, the hook's header and a bats comment had each recorded as a fact (that no option reaches log.showRoot) and found it false: a claim that a transform has no option is a claim about git, settled by running the option, not by the derivation's tags, and two rounds and a ruling had built on it unrun; and why r3 replaced an ENUMERATION with a derivation: the r2c closure listed the ways git calls a blob binary and closed the road by each attribute value, and the list was short by one key and two reserved words that real pushes found (the same class round r2 had ruled on for the credential half, a guard keyed on a name, a spelling or a value rather than on the property), so the identifier half now asks each read for its own verdict, and rounds r3c to r3f then asked, by real pushes over every post-image kind and merge shape the auditors could build, whether the derivation's scope equals the property's, the question a derived property owes and an enumeration cannot answer, and found and closed four gaps in it before the answer was yes; and why round 4 had the exit-0 class gated as a CLASS (every read whose empty or short answer would be read as nothing to judge, checked against a second read in hand) where round 3 had gated the numstat and check-attr alone, and had every arm's pin put its blob off the tip: a guarantee scoped to statuses left every exit-0 read a road of its own, four found by real pushes in one round, and a case the tip-owns rule decides is a pin that cannot red; the tags are the first cut and the disposition column above is the verdict.

A residual of a separate class, recorded here so the follow-up can take it, and re-read in round r7c against the committed hook. The nine JUDGE sites of the r5b text (its 1300, 1301, 1343, 1352, 1391, 1409, 1422, 1451 and 1460; 1079, 1080, 1118, 1127, 1155, 1170, 1183, 1212 and 1221 on the r3g text) were greps over captured text that branched the same way on no-match (status 1) and on a grep that could not run (status 2, or 127 off PATH), shown by execution with a grep shim exiting 2 ahead of the real one, a leaking tip, the denylist armed: ten `grep: simulated failure` lines and hook exit 0, where the real-grep control refused. CLOSED in r6 by the construction, not by the startup self-test this paragraph had proposed: every grep over captured content runs through judged_read with the expected set 0 and 1, so a status of 2 or 127 is refused as unscanned naming the read (the added-lines grep at 2037, the symlink-target grep at 1898, the address-domain greps at 2087 and 2201, the chosen-address match at 579, the message grep at 1735 for a commit and for a tag; each shown by a real push with a grep shim exiting 2: hook cases 175 and 176, identity 37 to 39, message 19 and 20, every one red at the r5b text), and the r5b text's two filters over the tip grep's output (its 1300 and 1301) and its two greps over the symlink listing (its 1342 and 1343) are bash loops now (1796-1803, 1864-1868), with no tool to fail. Round 6 then found the same greps' EXIT-0 silence open where the status alone had been read, the message grep and the added-lines grep, a match that printed no line read as no hit; closed in r7 (rows 1732-1744 and 2037-2045; hook 194, message 21 to 23). The tool-failure class over the hook's own scratch files, of the same shape and recorded since r3 and r5 as ended by errexit with no line (as_lines' tr and wc; the joins' awk and tr, the r5b text's 862, 865-869, 960, 1020 and 1057-1081; the population awks and the pcount read), is closed the same way: each runs through the helper and a tool that cannot run is refused naming it (as_lines' reads at 832 to 839; the joins at 1210, 1225, 1244, 1354, 1410 and 1478; the count's awk at 2444, case 185; the scanner log's sed and awk at 2486, 2491 and 2500; the sort at 2043; since r7 the tip's two count greps at 1248 and 1249 and the added lines' wc at 2015), the pcount awk alone staying a report read (1637, marked at its site); and the joins' EXIT-0 silence, open at the r6b text for the two joins whose answer is the candidate set (the round 6 refuters, real pushes under a silent awk and a silent tr publishing a hidden text file at the tip and in a middle commit: hook cases 195 to 198), is closed in r7 by the counts (rows 1225 and 1243-1257 and 1475-1503). The two counting greps r5 added are gone since r6 (the -z listing's count runs through the helper with the set 0 and 1, 1202, and its truly silent shape, the full count printed and status 2, is pinned since r7b by case 206 through a real push; the symlink pass counts in bash, 1867). What STANDS on the committed text, each with its witness: (1) the second class's BOUND, the ten own= reads, whose empty answer is declared the object's own at the call site and so outside the guarantee (the witness: the header's derived list, and case 174, which pins that the list is the tags and nothing else, red under four header mutants and, since r7b, red with the chosen-addresses tag removed from the list), stated at the header since r7 as the DERIVED BOUND with why a tool silent at each publishes nothing by itself: the ADDRESS DOMAIN grep (2087 and 2201), the CHOSEN ADDRESS match (579) and the SYMLINK TARGET grep (1898), three -q greps whose answer is their status alone (exit 0 is the hit, so no line is no answer withheld; a status outside 0 and 1 is refused, cases 176, 37 to 39); the REPLACE REFS listing (782), a git read alone whose empty answer is a clone with no replace ref (no tool between git and the loop, so the listing IS the fact; a git silent there is a git lying about its own refs, outside every guarantee the hook can give); the CHOSEN ADDRESSES read (569, r7b), a git read alone whose empty answer is a clone with no user.email, the strict side (every stamped address is then judged, so a git silent there excuses nothing; its failure is refused naming it, cases 171, 172 and 207); the TIP LISTING join against the read list (1210) and the LISTING join of a commit against its verdicts (1410), awks whose empty answer is the clean verdict of a gate on ANOTHER read, the -z listing against the read list and a commit's listing against its verdicts (a tool silent there disarms that one gate while the read it gates stands under its status and its other gates, the entry count at 1202 and the tip candidates count at 1248-1254, the numstats' own statuses and the join's count: shown by the round 6 refuters, extra8-4's evidence, that a silent shim on each alone publishes nothing); the TIP BLOBS of the -z listing (1225), whose emptied answer sends every per-commit candidate to the byte judge, the safe side (the hook's comment at 1220-1224); the ERR line of the scanner log (2491), whose empty answer is a log with no error (a silent awk there loses the error-line refusal while the COMMIT COUNT line beside it, gate=, refuses a shortened scan, and a shim silent on the count line is refused as no count); and the RANGE probe of the credential scan (2433), whose status alone is read, the answer discarded. The residual the bound leaves outside the guarantee, named at the header: two tools silent at once, a listing and its gate (a -z listing short AND the join that checks it silent; a commit's listing short AND its verdict join silent); and, the r7 hook fixer's FLAG 2 and the r7 audit's note 2, that four own= reads still run a tool (the three joins and the ERR line's awk) where ruling A's closing sentence reads "no external stage": the paragraph states the bound per read instead, a description and not a requirement in the fixer's and the auditor's reading, romp-manager's question; the rewrite in the shell (set membership over the scratch listings with associative arrays, a `read -r` loop over the log, about forty lines) is the other option, recoverable. (2) The twelve reads declared outside the helper (`outside judged_read:`), each with its reason at its site: the eight report reads inside the refused line (1566 the label's check-attr, 1575 the driver key, 1592 the blob's size, 1593 the threshold key, 1637 the parent count's awk over the hook's own pcount file, a read of no object, 1642 the rename listing and 1650 and 1656 the previous version's bytes in a parent and in each parent of a merge), which decide nothing (cases 168 to 170 and 187: a failed check-attr leaves the refusal standing with the tail open and both remedies printed); the one configuration read whose swallowed failure is the strict side (447, log.showRoot: case 173; the chosen-addresses read left this class in r7b, case 172 having shown its swallowed failure PUBLISH a clean-domain commit the read should have refused as unscanned); the ref list on stdin (465 to 467: the shell's own read since r7, no tool on PATH between git and the loops, a read error ending the hook under set -u, refusing the push; case 200); the scanner's own exit (2467: judged from its log, the eighth road's remedy); and the root-commit probe of the advice arm (2525: a line added to a refusal already made). Case 203 pins since r7b that no thirteenth read stands outside the helper undeclared (the marker count 10 lines at the r7b hook by the census's reading, since the ref list's and the scanner's declarations carry no tool word; 14 marker lines by grep, one the header's sentence and one a block declaration over the report's reads; the two shapes the census does not read, a backtick substitution and a second read after a call's `--`, are named by the r7b audit's note 2, neither used by the hook, romp-manager's question whether to name or close them). (3) The scratch directory left behind by a hard kill between its minting and its removal (row 2230; stated at the header; 0 leftovers in every round's runs under an exclusive TMPDIR). (4) The disclosed binary-to-text refusal with its two remedies (the identifier-half paragraph above: a refusal stated, not an open road; cases 110 to 114 and 186 to 189). (5) Three shapes outside every case and outside the rulings, recorded by the r7 rounds: a pusher's environment exporting POSIXLY_CORRECT makes a failed `exec 9>file` end the hook with bash's own line and no romp line, still refused, without the name (the r7 hook fixer's FLAG 6); the remedy paragraph under BLOCKED still opens with the configure-it sentence when the chosen-addresses read failed (the r7b hook fixer's FLAG 2, the r7b audit's note 1, romp-manager's question); and tests/pre-push-identity.bats' case for the CHOSEN ADDRESS match exiting 2 pins the address line's `not configured to use` for a refused match too, the same false-cause shape for is_chosen's own failure (the r7b hook fixer's FLAG 7, out of ruling C's scope). Nothing else of the judge or the tool-failure class remains on the committed text.
